### ✓ 2026-10-11: `concepts/radical-pair-magnetoreception` L56/L58 tells downstream pages to quote "tens of microseconds" (Gauger et al. 2011), but Gauger derive that figure from Ritz et al. 2009's narrow-band Larmor-frequency disruption, which Schwarze et al. 2016 did not replicate; 16 live files now carry the figure
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: /home/andy/unfin/unfinishablemap/obsidian/concepts/radical-pair-magnetoreception.md
- **Notes**: HEADROOM: analyze_length 2026-10-11: 2,061/3,500 (ample). VERIFIED BY THE SWEEP FORK: Gauger et al. 2011 (PRL 106:040503; arXiv 0906.3725) set their decay rate from their Ref. [10], Ritz et al. 2009 (*Biophys J* 96:3451; Frankfurt group), which reports a 1.316 MHz Larmor-frequency field at 150 nT disrupting the compass. Gauger's summary reads "given the widely-accepted RP model together with the recent experimental data [10], this conclusion follows." Schwarze et al. 2016 (PMID 27047356, Oldenburg) report that orientation "could not be disrupted by any of the relatively strong narrow-band electromagnetic fields employed here", including the Larmor frequency and 1.315 MHz. So the "at least tens of microseconds" requirement is conditional on a contested, unreplicated narrow-band result; the broadband-RF disruption results (replicated) bound coherence more loosely. Re-verify each of these at source before writing (Crossref/PubMed/arXiv), then re-scope L56/L58: the figure is Gauger's inference from the Ritz 2009 data, conditional on that result; state what the replicated broadband findings alone require (only if a source gives a figure, else say the bound is looser and unquantified). Change L58's "the number downstream Map articles should quote" accordingly. Do NOT edit the 16 downstream files in this pass; instead list them (grep `tens[- ]of[- ]microseconds` across topics/concepts/tenets/apex/voids/positions) in the changelog so a follow-on propagation task can be minted with the settled wording. Note that P-Q8's grade-B basis (behavioural broadband-RF disruption, in vitro sensitivity) does not depend on the narrow-band result. No "This is not X. It is Y."; no "load-bearing". ### ✓ 2026-10-11: `project/evidential-status-discipline` has no rule for when a *design* may be called "discriminating". The corpus names designs "discriminating tests" that discriminate only against the simplest production rival; add a short design-label clause requiring the lead to name the strongest rival the design does not exclude (outer-review 2026-10-11 ChatGPT, methodology improvements 1, 20, 21)
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: /home/andy/unfin/unfinishablemap/obsidian/project/evidential-status-discipline.md
- **Notes**: HEADROOM: the doc is already 22,372 words. Keep the addition to 150 words or fewer, and trim nearby if possible. **The gap.** The reviewer proposes that no experiment be labelled "discriminating" unless it names the strongest live rival, gives both models' prospective predictions, and says what observation would lower the Map model's probability. Designs should be graded against the strongest rival they survive, not the weakest they reject. The existing section "Compatibility vs. Support at the Rival-Model Interface" (L112–L120, the independent-discriminator requirement) governs how *evidence* is classified, not how *designs* are labelled. `apex/self-concealing-interface` L93 and L147 state the P(E | Map) > P(E | best rival) rule for seams. **Add** a design-label clause to that section. A design article may say "discriminates X from Y" only for the Y it names, and its lead must name the strongest rival the design does not exclude. A title may use "discriminating" only with that rival in view; titles may be qualified without slug changes. **Worked case to cite (do not edit it in this task):** `topics/direction-dependent-discriminating-test-design`, which on this review's verified counterexample discriminates only a static-threshold model; the P1 on that file is fixing it. Before naming `topics/targeted-lesion-discriminating-tests-between-production-and-filter-readings-of-the-memory-hierarchy` as a second case, read its lead (unverified here). Do not create a registry or tooling; reviewer improvement 23 (prediction registry) needs an operator decision.

### ✓ 2026-10-11: `topics/direction-dependent-discriminating-test-design` channel observables and premises. L60's four probes are presented as separable channel read-outs, but Dunn (2008) finds the remember/know task unidimensional across 37 studies, and Kim (2026) finds source memory familiarity-like. The sibling targeted-lesion caveat (L52) and the 2026-10-09 memory-channel recalibration (the ordering is "a hypothesis with exceptions"; cost is ranked "partly by the very ordering" it explains, L122) have not reached L58 or L86 (outer-review 2026-10-11 ChatGPT, verified)
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: /home/andy/unfin/unfinishablemap/obsidian/topics/direction-dependent-discriminating-test-design.md
- **Notes**: HEADROOM: analyze_length 2026-10-11 gives 2,474/4,000. Re-measure; two other tasks on this file run first. **Sources (verified 2026-10-11).** Dunn 2008, *Psychol Rev* 115(2):426, doi 10.1037/0033-295X.115.2.426 (PMID 18426296): a state-trace analysis of 37 studies found "little or no evidence of bidimensionality", and the paper concludes there is "insufficient evidence for the RK task to be used to identify qualitatively different memory components". Kim 2026, *Cereb Cortex*, doi 10.1093/cercor/bhag052 (PMID 42061891): source memory engages "familiarity-like, not recollection-like" patterns and is control-dependent. Sant'Anna, Michaelian & Andonovski 2024, *WIREs Cogn Sci*, doi 10.1002/wcs.1665: separates representational, metacognitive and epistemic accounts of autonoesis, so there is no agreed single target. **Fixes.** (1) L60: the probes measure behaviour on different scales (response format, chance level, reliability, decision criterion). Without a measurement model — fitted psychometric curves, matched formats where possible, reliability estimates — the order read off them is an order of behavioural thresholds, not latent channels. Bring in `topics/targeted-lesion-discriminating-tests-between-production-and-filter-readings-of-the-memory-hierarchy` L52's caveat (the probes "were not designed for tight cross-channel comparison under focal perturbation") with a link. (2) Probing in both phases trains the participant (retrieval practice, familiarity, strategy), so the second phase measures someone the first phase changed. Name this as a further physical route to a direction effect. (3) L58 "A high-cost target channel" and L86: propagate commit `37fc8c1630` (2026-10-09). `topics/memory-channel-interface-evidence` now calls the autonoetic-first ordering "a hypothesis with exceptions, not an established cross-condition finding". Its L122 concedes that "Bandwidth and reconstruction cost are ranked partly by the very ordering they are then invoked to explain". ### ✓ 2026-10-11: `topics/direction-dependent-discriminating-test-design` L56 treats tFUS as a reversible within-session ramp, but the cited Verhagen et al. (2019) protocol is offline, with effects "lasting up to 2 hr"; the design omits carry-over, the auditory confound (Kop et al. 2024), the Lord et al. (2024) active–sham null and unblinding, ITRUSST reporting, and Huang et al. (2021); L68's "reliably higher" is "usually higher" in Sepúlveda's abstract (outer-review 2026-10-11 ChatGPT, verified)
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: /home/andy/unfin/unfinishablemap/obsidian/topics/direction-dependent-discriminating-test-design.md
- **Notes**: HEADROOM: analyze_length 2026-10-11 gives 2,474/4,000. Re-measure after the P1 on this file, which runs first. **Sources (verified 2026-10-11 via Europe PMC).** (1) Verhagen et al. 2019, *eLife* 8:e40541, PMC6372282. Abstract: "modulates brain activation in macaques for more than one hour after 40 s of stimulation". Full text: "sustained over the duration of our experiment, lasting up to 2 hr". The article's "up to two hours … reversibly" is accurate, but the protocol is *offline*. There is no on-line ramp-down, and a reopening phase would run in tissue still carrying the closing-phase effect. L56's "The reversibility is the design's enabling feature: it supplies the reopening phase a lesion cannot" needs that caveat. The design then needs either a carry-over term or separate-session phases, at the cost of between-session variability. (2) Yaakub et al. 2023, *Nat Commun*, doi 10.1038/s41467-023-40998-0 (PMID 37658076): human theta-burst TUS to the PCC lowers GABA; changes "last at least 50 mins"; the authors raise "state- or location-dependency". (3) Kop et al. 2024, *eLife* 12:RP88762, doi 10.7554/eLife.88762: four experiments (one preregistered, three institutions) found TUS motor inhibition "was driven by peripheral auditory stimulation, not direct neuromodulation". A sound-matched active sham is therefore required. (4) Lord et al. 2024, *Front Hum Neurosci* 18:1392199, full text: "a single model that contrasted active and sham groups found no significant effects"; 11/15 active against 3/15 sham guessed "stimulation" (p = 0.0356); "it is very possible that the precuneus was also affected". L56 cites this only as the modality reaching the PCC. Link the caveats `topics/brain-stimulation-and-the-witness-mode` L49 already carries. (5) Martin et al. 2024, *Brain Stimulation*, doi 10.1016/j.brs.2024.04.013, "ITRUSST consensus on standardised reporting for transcranial ultrasound stimulation": cite it as the reporting standard, including in situ exposure estimates. (6) Huang et al. 2021, *NeuroImage*, doi 10.1016/j.neuroimage.2021.118042 (PMID 33848623), "Asymmetric neural dynamics characterize loss and recovery of consciousness". In humans under propofol, emergence shows "abrupt restoration" of cortical autocorrelation and subcorticocortical connectivity, and "Pharmacokinetic effects could not account for the difference". Add it to the hysteresis section (L66–L70) as independent evidence of a *physical* direction asymmetry; it strengthens the caution, not the filter reading. (7) L68: Sepúlveda, Tapia & Monsalves 2019, *Anaesthesia*, PMID 30835820. The abstract says "usually higher", so change "reliably" to "usually". Proekt & Hudson's abstract has "consistently lower" if a stronger word is wanted, cited there. **Shape:** recast tFUS as a candidate modality whose ramp-ability is unvalidated. Add a target-engagement precondition alongside L58's selectivity precondition. Stay concise; do not import a methods section. **Coordinate:** run after the P1 on this file. **HEADROOM UPDATE 2026-10-11 05:27Z (driver):** the P1 rewrite took this article from 2,474 to 3,408/4,000, leaving 591 words before the `>=` gate, SHARED by both open P2s on this file (tFUS L56 and the channel-observables/probes task). Budget accordingly, and reuse the P1's settled wording: "the static, equilibrated form of substrate-symmetric production", "static-threshold production model", "quasi-static, state-matched limit", and "excludes simple lag, but not multistability … or emergence-specific circuitry". The P1 added a sixth design ingredient, "Rate and dwell controls", so the ingredient count is now six. Line numbers in this task predate the P1; locate by quoted text.

It is OVER HARD, so the edit must be length-neutral or net-negative. **Defect 1: cross-article contradiction.** L141, *Magnitude / bound*: "the filter reading predicts a nonzero interaction—the autonoetic threshold crosses at a higher instantaneous substrate-function level on the falling leg than on the rising leg". The design article it summarises says the filter reading permits a dissociation without predicting one and forbids no ordering (L38, L48). The 2026-09-27 propagation refine `9ef0ee3e0e` fixed this apex's failure-condition line (L127) but left L141 alone; `git log -S'predicts a nonzero interaction'` shows only `f92041ee58` (2026-07-16). Alternatively, make it conditional on a named, specified interface model. **Defect 2: the comparator.** L141 says "the production reading predicts the interaction term is zero, because ordering is fixed by substrate state and not by trajectory". The protocol's own index, "instantaneous substrate-function level", rescues the comparator only if that level is measured per channel at equilibrium. If channel time constants are intrinsic to the channels (task or representational integration times), a lag model gives the same sign across agents. Cross-agent invariance is then not "exactly the manipulation a production account expects to dissolve it" (L147). This is a verifier inference to weigh, not a demonstrated result. At minimum, require the invariance to hold in the quasi-static limit. Also note that L127 already lists "a single substrate-state mechanism that derives both the rising-trajectory and falling-trajectory orderings without per-case additions" as a failure condition, and the lag model is a candidate for it. **Coordinate:** run after the P1 on `topics/direction-dependent-discriminating-test-design` from the same review, and match the wording it settles.

### ✓ 2026-10-11: `topics/direction-dependent-discriminating-test-design` says substrate-symmetric production "forbids" a non-mirror channel order, but a direction-blind, fixed-threshold model with unequal channel time constants produces "fails first, recovers first" under the article's own matched-rate ramps; L70's Proekt & Hudson clause and the Tenet paragraphs (L84, L86) overclaim the same way (outer-review 2026-10-11 ChatGPT, verified; convergent with Gemini, synthesis 2026-10-11)
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: /home/andy/unfin/unfinishablemap/obsidian/topics/direction-dependent-discriminating-test-design.md
- **Notes**: From convergent outer reviews (2026-10-11, 2/3 reviewers; the Claude leg was not commissioned, weekly-limit backoff to 2026-10-14): [chatgpt, gemini]. Already P1, so not upgraded. Two clusters land in this task. (C1, the "forbids" defect) ChatGPT: "the article's claim that the ordering is 'forbidden' by substrate-symmetric production fails"; Gemini: "Premise 2 is demonstrably false in any physical system exhibiting hysteresis, plasticity, or metastability. The brain is not a memoryless, Markovian system". The two reviewers name different mechanisms, unequal lags (ChatGPT) and attractor-basin path dependence (Gemini). The quasi-static control in the fix rules out the first only, so the rewritten L74 must leave multistability standing. (C2, emergence bookkeeping at L50/L74) ChatGPT: "its evidential interpretation still charges physicalism for a mechanism whose existence is independently motivated"; Gemini: "the brain does not use the identical neural circuitry to wake up as it does to fall asleep". Gemini audited a composite of several Map pages, so use ONLY its §3–4, and only on the points the CONVERGENT clause below admits. Synthesis: reviews/outer-review-synthesis-2026-10-11.md. HEADROOM: analyze_length 2026-10-11 gives 2,474/4,000, so 1,525 words to hard. Re-measure before editing; the gate is `>=`. **The counterexample, with the arithmetic checked.** Take τᵢ q̇ᵢ = p − qᵢ. A channel fails when qᵢ > θᵢ, and the same law runs in both directions. At ramp rate r, the up-crossing is p ≈ θᵢ + rτᵢ and the down-crossing p ≈ θᵢ − rτᵢ. For A (θ 0.40, τ 0.10) and B (θ 0.45, τ 0.20) at r = 1, A fails first (0.50 vs 0.65) and recovers first (0.30 vs 0.25 as p falls). The model is direction-blind with one threshold per channel, yet it produces the "clean reversal" the article says this reading forbids. Matched rates (L62) equalise r, not τᵢ. None of the 22 earlier reviews of this article raised it. **Loci:** description L3 ("forbidden by substrate-symmetric production"); lead L38 ("predicts mirror symmetry and forbids its failure"); L44; L46 ("**forbids** a clean reversal"); L50 ("It does, *for the substrate-symmetric reading*"); L62; L64 ("predicts a single threshold per channel, crossed in both directions" — true of the internal threshold, false of the crossing indexed to the external setting); L74 ("would remove the substrate-symmetric production reading"). **Fix.** Restrict the forbidding to a *static or quasi-static (equilibrated)* threshold model, and say why: a law that is the same in both directions does not give time-reversed trajectories in a dissipative system. Add the lag countermodel briefly, as the minimum rival any design must beat. Make two controls part of the design. First, a ramp-rate manipulation: a reversal that shrinks as r → 0 is lag. Second, long dwell times with independently verified equilibration at each level: a reversal that survives equilibration rules out simple lag, but not multistability or active emergence circuitry. The discriminating read-out becomes a reversal that persists in the quasi-static limit at matched *measured* neural state, not matched administered intensity. L74 must then say what a clean reversal removes: the static-threshold model, not "substrate-symmetric production" as a whole. **L70 (Proekt & Hudson).** "being direction-blind in its cross-channel ordering, does not predict" goes beyond the source. The 2018 model (BJA 121(1):86–94, PMC6200107) has awake and unconscious states only, with no memory channels. Say the paper is silent on channel order. Add that a multistate transition graph in which channels read different latent states can produce rank changes, and name the restriction that would prevent them. Keep the ten-state framing (2026-06-20 and 2026-09-27 stability notes). **Tenet paragraphs.** L84's "the design treats the interface reading as empirically exposed" overclaims. The filter reading forbids no ordering (L38, L48), so what is exposed is the evidence family, not the theory. The paragraph's last sentence already narrows to "one of the Map's evidence types"; make the opening match it. L86's "the predicted direction effect is largest on the autonoetic channel" brings back a filter-side prediction that the 2026-09-27 stability note forbids ("Do not reintroduce 'the filter reading predicts asymmetry'"). Restate it as the design's working assumption, and add that "subject-participation" has no independent measure. **Stability:** keep the one-sided framing, and do NOT make the filter reading predict asymmetry. Title: may stay. If you qualify it, do not change the slug. **CONVERGENT (Gemini 2.5 Pro 2026-10-11, `reviews/outer-review-2026-10-11-gemini-2-5-pro.md` §3–4, verified):** a second reviewer independently says a history-dependent system need not retrace its path, so the "forbids" fix above now has two reviewers behind it. Gemini also raises one locus this task must cover. L50 lists "a distinct emergence-circuit posit" among the machinery an elaborate production reading must "pay" for, and L74 charges a reversal-absorbing production account "the per-case cost". But emergence-specific circuitry is documented, not posited. Kelz et al. 2008, *PNAS* 105(4):1309–1314, doi 10.1073/pnas.0707146105: orexin ablation "delays emergence from anesthesia, without changing anesthetic induction". `concepts/active-reboot` L73 already quotes Mashour (2024), that exit "is not a simple mirror image" of entry. Reword L50 so that independently evidenced emergence machinery is not booked as a paid addition, and limit L74's cost clause to machinery introduced to absorb this result. Do NOT cite PVH-CRH neurons (Jiang et al. 2024) as emergence-specific: they shift induction and emergence both. Do NOT use Gemini's "Kim et al. (2021)" neural-inertia citation; that paper is Friedman et al. 2010, *PLoS One*, PMID 20689589. **Coordinate:** run this before the sibling P1 on `apex/self-concealing-interface` L141 and the P2s on this file and on `concepts/direction-of-interface-change`; they reuse the wording this task settles.

### ✓ 2026-10-11: Propagate the 2026-10-11 Tollock et al. (2025) source-fidelity correction to five sibling articles — they say the prospective events were "predominantly brief and trigger-linked" with a 4.1% "terminal" subtype, and the cited abstract reports neither
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/consciousness-interface-development.md
- **Notes**: The deep review of `topics/terminal-lucidity-and-filter-transmission-theory` (2026-10-11) web-verified the Tollock, Leontovich, Gonzalez & Parnia (2025) GSA abstract at Crossref (DOI 10.1093/geroni/igaf122.2914, raw JATS abstract) and found the corpus's characterization unsupported. VERIFIED ABSTRACT TEXT: Results = "Of 151, 93 (61.6%) reported lucidity, with 267 distinct events that were consistent with reality: 1) appropriate orientation to events (67.8%), 2) return of old memories (34.8%), 3) return of functional abilities (27.7%), 4) nonverbal communication (25.1%), 5) terminal lucidity (memories consistent with near-death experiences) (4.1%). ... Potential triggers to PL episodes were identified: music, anniversaries, emotional distress, and medication changes, including immunomodulating agents and antihistamines." Events were logged by primary caregivers ("perceived mental clarity"); all patients had life expectancy < 12 months. The network-level quote is from the abstract's BACKGROUND ("highlights the possibility of a network-level return of cognitive function despite severe dementia"), i.e. the authors' framing of the phenomenon, NOT a finding. DEFECTS: (a) the abstract reports NO durations, so "brief" is not a Tollock finding (note Batthyány & Greyson 2021's retrospective median was already 30–60 min, so brevity does not distinguish the prospective record); (b) NO severity grading, so "mild"/"modest" is unsupported; (c) NO timing relative to death, so "non-terminal" is unsupported; (d) the 4.1% category is defined by near-death-experience-like CONTENT, not proximity to death, so "only 4.1% met a terminal-lucidity definition / the dramatic terminal subtype" misreads it; (e) "trigger-linked" overstates "potential triggers were identified" (no share of episodes is reported); (f) "the first prospective study" is contested — MacLeod 2009 (*Palliative & Supportive Care* 7(4):513–516) prospectively observed 100 hospice deaths, and Gilmore-Bykovskyi et al. 2025 (*The Gerontologist* 65(9), gnaf178, DOI 10.1093/geront/gnaf178) prospectively video-recorded 20 advanced-dementia participants (nine validated episodes in three of them across 539 observations; "marked recovery of verbal communication abilities was a defining feature across all events"). LOCI: `topics/consciousness-interface-development` L95 ("predominantly mild and non-terminal") + L99 ("Only 4.1% ... met a terminal-lucidity definition ... mostly brief, trigger-linked episodes"); `apex/altered-states-as-interface-evidence` L128 ("the first prospective study ... events are predominantly brief and trigger-linked ... terminal subtype only 4.1% ... frame the finding"); `concepts/near-death-experiences` L142 (same triple); `concepts/filter-theory` L113 (same triple); `concepts/mind-brain-separation` L86 ("The first prospective study found only 4.1% ... most of them brief and trigger-linked"). Also annotate (do not rewrite) the dated research notes `research/terminal-lucidity-filter-theory-2026-03-20` L19/L22/L178/L207 and `research/intact-substrate-reversibility-2026-09-16` L234/L255. MODEL WORDING: copy the corrected paragraph in `topics/terminal-lucidity-and-filter-transmission-theory` "Modern Systematic Data" ¶2 and level 3 of "Separating the Levels". CALIBRATION — DO NOT OVER-SWING: what survives is that the prospective data confirms the episodes, records no neural activity, and its one mechanistic lead (medication triggers) is a physical route, so the prospective evidence "does not point toward" the filter reading; drop "points away from" only where it rested on the brief/mild/4.1% descriptors. LENGTH: the apex and several concepts pages run near their gates — measure each with `analyze_length` first and make each fix length-neutral (the corrected sentences are no longer than the originals). Bump `ai_modified` on each file touched.

### ✓ 2026-10-10: `concepts/types-of-consciousness` frames higher-order theory as a "monitoring" layer added to an independent phenomenal layer, and runs Block's AI conclusion without the HOT/PRM rival (outer-review 2026-10-10 Gemini)

- **File**: /home/andy/unfin/unfinishablemap/obsidian/concepts/types-of-consciousness.md
- **Notes**: The reviewer's absence claim is FALSE at corpus level: `concepts/higher-order-theories` covers Brown's HOROR (L83–L85), Lau's Perceptual Reality Monitoring (L87) and LeDoux (L75), and this article links to it. Two narrower defects are real in this file. (1) L57 says "**Monitoring consciousness** adds a third dimension … Higher-order theories of consciousness (Rosenthal 2005) hold that a state becomes conscious when a higher-order representation takes it as an object. This introduces the possibility of consciousness that is phenomenal and access-available but not self-monitored — experience without awareness of experiencing." On Rosenthal's view the HOT is what makes a state conscious at all; it is usually non-conscious, and only *introspective* (consciously self-aware) states need a further, conscious higher-order state. So HOT does not introduce "phenomenal but not self-monitored" experience as a possibility built on an independent P layer. What it allows is conscious experience without *introspective* awareness. State that precisely, and do not cite HOT as support for a free-standing P layer, which it denies. (2) L49 runs Block's claim that functional AI might have "rich A-consciousness … while having zero P-consciousness" with Dennett as the only dissent noted. HOT and PRM are the competing framework that predicts the opposite for a system with the right higher-order architecture. Name that rival in a sentence and point to `higher-order-theories`. The AI section's verdict, "unlikely for current architectures", does not need to change. **Do NOT use the reviewer's citation** "LeDoux, J. E., et al. (2023). 'Emotion, Consciousness, and the Brain.' *Neuron*": it does not resolve in Crossref (checked 2026-10-10). Use the anchors already in `higher-order-theories`' reference list. The reviewer's "self-HOROR" and "General Network of Cognition" glosses are unverified; do not import them. Apply the direct-refutation discipline. Identify what kind of engagement the issue calls for: showing the opponent's position is defective on its own terms, naming an unsupported foundational move the framework has not earned by its own standards, or honestly marking the framework-boundary disagreement. Apply the corresponding reply mode in **natural journal-quality prose** — see [[writing-style|the writing-style guide]]'s "Engaging Opponents in Journal-Quality Prose" section. **Do not expose mode labels in the article body.** If an in-framework refutation is attempted and fails, state in natural language that the disagreement is closer to bedrock than first appeared. No "This is not X. It is Y."; no "load-bearing". Set ai_modified from `date -u`; changelog before sync; sync; verify both trees. - **Type**: refine-draft

- **Notes**: TWO files: (a) /home/andy/unfin/unfinishablemap/obsidian/topics/anaesthesia-and-the-consciousness-interface.md and (b) /home/andy/unfin/unfinishablemap/obsidian/apex/identity-across-transformations.md. **(a)** L69 reads "Propofol and xenon both abolish phenomenal experience — patients report nothing, no dreams, no sense of time." The article's own L95 ("in some protocols nearly all unresponsive participants recalled dreaming on emergence") and L117 ("Disconnected consciousness … is common during emergence") contradict the categorical claim. **Source** (Europe PMC abstract, retrieved 2026-10-10): Valli, K., Radek, L., Kallionpää, R. "Subjective experiences during dexmedetomidine- or propofol-induced unresponsiveness and non-rapid eye movement sleep in healthy male subjects." *British Journal of Anaesthesia*, doi 10.1016/j.bja.2023.04.026. Of 76 interviews after anaesthetic-induced unresponsiveness, 69.7% included experiences, with no difference between dexmedetomidine and propofol. Disconnected dream-like experience was common and connected awareness rare. **Keep Sarasso et al. (2015)'s datum**: in their protocol nothing was reported after propofol or xenon, unlike ketamine. That is accurate. The defect is the step from absent report to abolished experience. Rewrite L69/L71 so the propofol/xenon contrast concerns absent *reports* at Sarasso's depth, and treat extinction as one of the three states the article later distinguishes (unconsciousness, disconnected, connected). Dose caveat: Valli's subjects were at the threshold of unresponsiveness, not surgical depth, so do not claim propofol preserves experience at every depth. Do NOT add the reviewer's "22 of 30 under propofol": it is not in the abstract. (a) must be length-neutral. Gemini named this article for reading 2025 consciousness-science results in the Map's favour ("smoothly pivoting to assert that physicalist models are inherently failing"; "Production theories of physicalism explicitly predict exactly this kind of covert consciousness"), verified here at L106 and L110. ChatGPT independently found the same callosal result over-read in the sibling `topics/split-brain-consciousness` ("Those results strengthen the causal role of residual physical connectivity. They do not show unity persisting independently of connectivity."). Four articles now read Santander et al. (2025) two ways: this article (L72, L74, L110) and split-brain-consciousness (L72, L76, L122) for the interface or for unity; `concepts/neural-correlates-of-consciousness` L118 and `apex/identity-across-transformations` L99 ("the spared ribbon is doing the work") for production. The split-brain task (P1, same synthesis) brings that article into line. Use the NCC L118 / identity L99 reading as the shared target so both fixes land on the same wording; if either executor judges that reading wrong, flag it in the changelog rather than diverging. Synthesis: reviews/outer-review-synthesis-2026-10-10.md. **One study, two opposite verdicts.** This article's L74 says "The finding is more naturally explained if physical connections serve as an *interface* for consciousness rather than *constituting* it", and L110 says "The split-brain findings and covert consciousness evidence are more naturally read on the Map's [[tenets#^bidirectional-interaction|Bidirectional Interaction]] tenet". Against that, `concepts/neural-correlates-of-consciousness` L118 reads the same Santander et al. (2025) PNAS result as "Callosal evidence leans toward production … unity tracks a surviving physical channel". NCC L116 counts covert consciousness as part of "one underdetermined pattern", with the accommodation move "symmetrically available to the production theorist". NCC L156 says "persistence without output shows nothing about consciousness acting on the brain, so lends [[tenets#^bidirectional-interaction|Bidirectional Interaction]] no support." The NCC wording is the later, more calibrated reading (its falsifier rewrite is commit d1b509ca5d, W40). Bring this article into line with it, or, if you judge NCC wrong, say so in the changelog and flag it; do not leave the two silently divergent. **Specific repairs.** (1) L110: inbound-interface evidence (unity through a narrow channel, experience behind a damaged output channel) bears at most on the interface-or-generator question. It cannot support Bidirectional Interaction, which concerns consciousness acting on the brain. Re-home the claim or withdraw it. (2) L72 asserts that "On standard computational models, losing 99% of cross-module bandwidth would typically produce proportional degradation in system performance, not binary preservation of full function". This is unsupported, because a small channel suffices for many integration tasks. Either source it or cut it, and keep L74's interface reading only as one option that the production reading accommodates equally. (3) L106 (the COGITATE paragraph, "these explanations do not yet work"; also the lead at L38, "the results are striking"): this is the reviewer's verified calibration point. COGITATE challenged two specific theories (IIT and GNWT); it did not test physicalism as such, and the Map's interface model made no prediction COGITATE could have tested. State the comparison symmetrically. NCC's "What Would Challenge This View?" section (L137–L141: "Reachable, not discriminating") is the model to follow. Keep the article's existing honest close at L112 ("none discriminates the interface model from its physicalist rivals"). ### ✓ 2026-10-10: Pitts (2022) is cited for the reverse of its conclusion at three sites: `topics/selection-only-mind-influence` L123, `topics/causal-closure-debate-historical-survey` L101, `concepts/selection-only-channel` L106 (outer-review 2026-10-10 Gemini, verified at source)
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: /home/andy/unfin/unfinishablemap/obsidian/topics/selection-only-mind-influence.md
- **Notes**: THREE files: /home/andy/unfin/unfinishablemap/obsidian/topics/selection-only-mind-influence.md, /home/andy/unfin/unfinishablemap/obsidian/topics/causal-closure-debate-historical-survey.md and /home/andy/unfin/unfinishablemap/obsidian/concepts/selection-only-channel.md. **The source, from the Crossref abstract retrieved 2026-10-10:** Pitts, J. B. (2022), "General Relativity, Mental Causation, and Energy Conservation", *Erkenntnis* 87(4), 1931–1973, doi 10.1007/s10670-020-00284-7. It says that "a few authors have proposed that the non-localizability of gravitational energy and consequent lack of physically meaningful local conservation laws answers the conservation objection", and then that the paper "uses the generalized Bianchi identities to show that General Relativity tends to exclude, not facilitate, such Cartesian mental causation. In the simplest case, Cartesian mental influence must be spatio-temporally constant, and hence 0. The difficulty may diminish for more complicated models." Pitts 2020 (*Philosophia* 48, 673–707; Europe PMC abstract) ends the same way: "far from providing a loophole, General Relativity makes mental causation harder." So the Map credits Pitts with the position his paper was written to refute. **The three sites, located by QUOTED old text (confirm each occurs once and print the live line):** (a) selection-only-mind-influence: "As Robin Collins and J. B. Pitts have pointed out (Collins, n.d.; Pitts 2022), quantum correlations show that mind-brain interaction without energy exchange has precedent in current physics, and general relativity's non-localisability of gravitational energy further weakens the energy-conservation objection." Remove the GR clause's attribution to Pitts and state Pitts 2022's result as a constraint the channel still owes an answer to. Also check whether the "quantum correlations … precedent" half is in Pitts 2022 at all; the abstract does not support it, so credit Collins alone unless a page cite is found. (b) causal-closure-debate-historical-survey: "Separately, as Pitts (2022) and Cucu and Pitts (2019) have argued, energy is not straightforwardly conserved in general relativity or in the universe as a whole, so insisting on energy conservation specifically for brain processes requires justification that physicalists have not provided." The global-sum and begs-the-question points are Pitts 2020's and are fine ("Constant total energy holds if the global summing-up of local conservation laws converges; it probably doesn't in reality"). The GR non-conservation clause attributed to Pitts 2022 is reversed: Pitts 2022 is the GR objection, not the GR loophole. (c) selection-only-channel: "which is why the energy-conservation objection to mental causation does not apply to it (Collins n.d.; Pitts 2022)". Pitts 2022 does not argue this. Cite the conditional/local-conservation point to Pitts 2020 or Cucu & Pitts 2019, or name Pitts 2022 as the open GR constraint. **A fair statement for all three sites:** Pitts's Bianchi-identity argument targets mental influence entering the field equations as a source. Whether a strictly selection-only channel that adds no stress-energy escapes it is open, and the Map should say so without claiming Pitts for either side ("The difficulty may diminish for more complicated models" is his hedge, not an endorsement). **Do not adopt the reviewer's "if not mathematically impossible"**, which goes beyond both abstracts. Prior deep reviews "verified" Pitts 2022 on metadata only (deep-review-2026-06-17-selection-only-channel L47; deep-review-2026-04-23-conservation-laws-and-mental-causation L47); they are not reading-verifications. optimistic-2026-07-27-causal-exclusion-closure-cluster L135 proposed amplifying the reversed reading; do NOT install that. After the fix, grep the whole corpus (including archive/) for "Pitts 2022" / "Pitts (2022)" / "non-localisab" and report any further site. No "This is not X. It is Y."; no "load-bearing". Set ai_modified from `date -u` on each touched file; changelog before sync; `uv run python scripts/sync.py`; verify every touched file in BOTH trees. The four-levels rule (L268–280) stages separability → phenomenological reality → mechanistic independence → metaphysical irreducibility, but says nothing about what "intact substrate" is allowed to mean, and the clinical wing's flagship article walked up an unmarked ladder from "no gross destructive lesion" (true) through "no atrophy" (false for DID: Dimitrova et al. 2021 hippocampal/CA1 volumes) to "the substrate stays whole". Add, dated 2026-10-08: (a) **the absence ladder** — an intact-substrate premise must name its rung: no focal destructive lesion / no conventional-MRI abnormality / no group-level structural difference / no microstructural, neurochemical or connectivity change / no physical cause — and may only claim the rung the cited evidence reaches; the default phrasing for the clinical wing is "no focal destructive lesion sufficient to explain the symptom"; (b) **source-type labelling** — when a sentence rests an empirical finding on a citation, the citation must be of the type that can generate that finding (primary experiment / case series / systematic review / narrative review / theoretical model / scale-development paper); the exhibit is Sierra & Berrios (1998), a theoretical model, standing where Phillips et al. (2001), the n=6 fMRI study, belongs — and a *verbatim* quotation does not certify the inference drawn from it (inference fidelity is a separate check from quotation fidelity; Marsh et al. 2021 "may be available for retrieval" is the exhibit). ### ✓ 2026-10-08: `topics/clinical-dissociation-as-systematic-evidence` — intact-substrate ladder (L64 "no atrophy", L139 "substrate stays whole"), Sierra & Berrios 1998 cited where Phillips 2001 is the imaging source (L88), Hassa PPI read as direction (L110), amnesia recovery overclaim (L102), dangling Anderson & Hanslmayr (L98), sedation not anaesthesia (L106) — LENGTH-NEUTRAL (4,694 over the 4,000 ceiling; standing human length decision)
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: /home/andy/unfin/unfinishablemap/obsidian/topics/clinical-dissociation-as-systematic-evidence.md
- **Notes**: From convergent outer reviews (2026-10-08, 2/3 reviewers): [chatgpt, claude] — already P1, not upgraded further. Eight verified defects, all inference-fidelity or source-type, none a fabricated quotation. Make each fix at the named locus; do not restructure.
  (1) **Intact-substrate ladder.** L56 already has the defensible form ("undamaged, not unchanged"; "no gross destructive lesion is required"). L64 escalates to "no lesion, no atrophy, no pharmacological blockade" and "without anything being broken"; L68 to "without any substrate change"; L139 to "the substrate stays whole". Dimitrova et al. (2021, *Psychol Med* 53:805–813, doi 10.1017/S0033291721002154) found smaller bilateral hippocampal and CA1 volumes in 32 DID vs 43 controls, with dissociative amnesia uniquely correlated with reduced CA1 — so "no atrophy" is false as a categorical claim about DID (smaller volume is not atrophy, but it is a group-level structural difference). Rewrite L64 to "no focal destructive lesion sufficient to explain the symptom" + one clause conceding group-level structural differences in DID (cite Dimitrova); change L68 "without any substrate change" → "without a lesion to drive it"; change L139 "while the substrate stays whole" → "without a destructive lesion". Add Dimitrova to References.
  (2) **Sierra & Berrios → Phillips.** L88: Sierra & Berrios (1998) is a theoretical corticolimbic-disconnection model (no imaging). The imaging result the sentence leans on is Phillips et al. (2001, *Psychiatry Res* 108:145–160, doi 10.1016/s0925-4927(01)00119-6): 6 DPD / 10 OCD / healthy controls; aversive scenes produced reduced activation in the insula **and occipito-temporal cortex** with increased right ventral PFC. So "while perceptual cortex functions normally" is contradicted by the actual source — delete that clause, keep S&B as the model, cite Phillips for the finding with its n=6, and note the occipito-temporal reduction. Add Phillips to References.
  (3) **Direction language.** L110: Hassa et al. (2017) used PPI, which is undirected — "replicated the direction" → "replicated the increased limbic–motor coupling (PPI, undirected) in functional hemiparesis". Voon et al. (2010) Granger result was significant for happy faces only (trend for fearful): "with amygdala activity predicting subsequent motor-area changes rather than the reverse" → "with exploratory Granger modelling pointing amygdala→SMA (significant for happy faces only)". Length-neutral.
  (4) **Amnesia recovery overclaim.** L102 asserts "autobiographical memories typically return as a body rather than being relearned piecemeal" and "recovery is reconnection, not reconstruction", citing Staniloiu & Markowitsch (2014) — a *Lancet Psychiatry* Review that cannot carry a frequency claim, and L98 has just conceded Mangiulli et al.'s point that most cases fail to rule out ordinary forgetting/non-encoding. Rewrite as: reversible cases are *reported*, sometimes abrupt; whether recovery is retrieval of retained content or cue-dependent reconstruction is open (the article's own L96 list). Delete "typically return as a body" and "reconnection, not reconstruction". Delete the terminal-lucidity analogy clause in the same paragraph (review item 40 — one disputed evidence base propping another). L98 "the hard drive is damaged" → "the trace or its retrieval machinery is damaged" (length-neutral).
  (5) **Dangling citation.** L98 "Anderson and Hanslmayr's motivated-forgetting work" has no reference-list entry. Add: Anderson, M. C., & Hanslmayr, S. (2014). Neural mechanisms of motivated forgetting. *Trends in Cognitive Sciences*, 18(6), 279–292. https://doi.org/10.1016/j.tics.2014.03.002 — and add "laboratory" before "retrieval-suppression" so trained-item suppression is not equated with years of autobiographical loss.
  (6) **Sedation, not anaesthesia.** L106 "Under anaesthesia or hypnosis, the 'paralysed' limb may move normally" flattens the sibling's grade (`conversion-disorder-as-consciousness-side-fault` L67: Stone et al. 2014, eleven patients under propofol *sedation*, five cured or much improved; hypnosis evidence very thin). → "Under propofol sedation (a small uncontrolled series) or hypnosis, the 'paralysed' limb has been reported to move."
  (7) **Reinders gloss + currency.** L80 calls the rCBF result "the connectivity-pattern signature the channel reading predicts" — rCBF is regional activity, not connectivity: → "the state-dependent-access signature". L82: add one clause after Marsh — Beker, Dorahy, Moir & Cording (2024, *Clin Psychol Rev* 114:102514, doi 10.1016/j.cpr.2024.102514; systematic review, 2 of 4 meta-analyses supported inter-identity transfer, high heterogeneity) — and add to References. L80 "semantic knowledge and procedural skills typically transfer" → "semantic knowledge and procedural skills are usually reported to transfer". Optional if budget allows: Vissia et al. (2022, *BJPsych Open* 8:e82) identity-state-dependent working memory complicates "lower channels carry on".
  (8) **Grade words.** L90 and L131 "fully specified physicalist rival" → "mechanistically articulated physicalist rival" (Seth 2012 is a Hypothesis & Theory paper; Ciaunica 2022 a theoretical synthesis). L141: after "would separate them" add the divergent prediction in ≤12 words ("— production predicting a changed ordering, filter the standard one") so the discriminator matches the dedicated test article. Table L118: rename column "Connectivity Change" → "Mechanism (measured or modelled)"; no new columns.
  **Trim list (pay for every addition from here; ~200 words available):** L62 "Random neural degradation would produce random experiential degradation." (straw man, −9); L139 "A binary 'consciousness on/off' production theory cannot reach the data: it predicts uniform impairment, and dissociation is the opposite of uniform." (review item 24, −22); L102 terminal-lucidity clause (−25); L133 second sentence duplicating L56's GWT/IIT accommodation (−30); L157 "This is not fairly read as ad-hoc epicycle-adding … independently motivated." duplicates L131 (−35); L125 "The disconnections are selective, predictable, and map onto the same interface architecture…" (−20, the table already says it); L141 "Neither gets the connectivity geometry for free;" (−8); L74 "EEG and fMRI studies reveal measurable differences between alter states—different autonomic responses, different neural activation patterns, different perceptual processing—yet" → "Imaging studies reveal measurable differences between alter states, yet" (−14, and it was unsourced). Sync both trees after; confirm `hugo/content/topics/` matches.
  **Convergent (Claude Fable 5.1, 2026-10-08 — `reviews/outer-review-2026-10-08-claude-fable-5-1.md` §4):** items (1)–(7) above are independently flagged (L64/L68/L139 ladder; L88 "perceptual cortex functions normally" — Claude adds Simeon et al. 2000 *Am J Psychiatry* 157:1782 PET, PMID 11058475, as a second contradicting source; L110 Hassa PPI + Voon happy-only/positive-symptom qualifiers; L102 recovery + terminal-lucidity; L98 Anderson & Hanslmayr; L106 sedation; L80 Reinders gloss + Beker). Do these first. Then, in order, as budget allows (all substitutions unless marked +):
  (9) **Body-to-surface propagation** (the defect both reviewers name: the body concedes, the surfaces don't). L3 `description:` — replace with: "Dissociative disorders—DID, depersonalisation, conversion, amnesia—selectively alter access, ownership, memory and motor control without a focal lesion, through decreased coupling in three and *increased* coupling in conversion; a pattern compatible with a dualist interface architecture without favouring it over predictive-processing physicalism." (frontmatter, free). L118 column "What Disconnects" → "What Is Altered" (0). L161 Further-Reading gloss "six altered states converging on the same multi-channel interface architecture" → the apex's own 2026-10-02 wording: "six altered states whose one shared pattern constrains consciousness-brain coupling without deciding between filter and production readings" (+7; the apex was recalibrated after this gloss was written). Do NOT retitle — the title/URL change is a human decision, recorded in the review's Verification Notes.
  (10) **DID opening vs objective transfer.** L74 "Distinct personality states maintain separate experiential streams, autobiographical memories, and self-concepts within a single anatomically intact brain" → "Identity states *report* separate autobiographical access and distinct self-concepts within one anatomically intact brain; on objective tests information transfers between them (see Marsh et al. below)" (≈+6). L78 "the partition falls along the autonoetic channel rather than scrambling memory systems indiscriminately" → "the *reported* partition falls along the autonoetic channel, with objective retention underneath, rather than scrambling memory systems indiscriminately" (+4). If budget allows, add Donath, van der Linde, Verschuere et al. (2025) *Clin Psychol Rev* 121:102638, doi 10.1016/j.cpr.2025.102638 (corrected re-analysis of the Beker meta-analyses) after Beker at L82 (+~20 incl. reference). Vissia 2022 stays optional.
  (11) **L92 "recurrent" factor.** Simeon, Kozin, Segal & Lerch (2008) *Psychiatry Res* 157:303–306, doi 10.1016/j.psychres.2007.07.007, PMID 17959254 (n=394) extracted five CDS factors with no separate recall factor, so "isolate a recurrent factor" → "isolate a factor (not reproduced in Simeon et al.'s larger 2008 analysis)"; and "distinct from any failure of content retrieval" → "reported as distinct from any felt failure of content retrieval (no objective retrieval test was run)" (+~12; add the 2008 reference, +~20). Also L88: "extend this to DPDR specifically" → "give DPDR its own active-inference account" (0), and the DPDR header L84 "Goes Silent" → "Is Inhibited" (Sierra & Berrios 1998 is active prefrontal inhibition inside a corticolimbic-disconnection frame; the article's "disconnection" gloss is theirs, but "silent" is not).
  (12) **L127 residual-independence rescue.** "[[voids/anesthesia-void|pharmacological dissociation]] add further routes that do not reduce to the precision-weighting family" is the article's single most dated claim: ketamine dissociation is standardly modelled as disrupted top-down prediction — Wehrman et al. (2023) *Br J Anaesth* 131:705–714, doi 10.1016/j.bja.2023.06.044, PMID 37541951 ("subanaesthetic doses of ketamine reduce but do not eliminate predictive coding responses"); Vesuna et al. (2020) *Nature* 586:87–94 for the retrosplenial rhythm; Laukkonen, Friston & Chandaria (2025) *Neurosci Biobehav Rev* 176:106296 for the unifying active-inference theory. Rewrite the clause so the pharmacological route's independence is *contested rather than assumed*, discount the compound signature one notch further ("the routes' residual independence is weaker than four-fold" → "…is weaker than four-fold, and the pharmacological route's independence is itself disputed"), cite Wehrman 2023 (+~25 incl. reference). Calibration: this LOWERS the tier; nothing may rise.
  (13) **L78 register consistency.** After "the alters may be different configurations of the interface rather than different minds" add: "— though by the Map's own [[positions/individuation-and-subjecthood|P-I3]] the brain's physical singleness cannot settle whether DID involves one subject or several" (+~22). This is the Claude review's one internal-consistency finding that is not already in the body; the companion positions-evolve task adds the register side.
  (14) Optional, last: L147 "the mind protecting itself by severing access to unbearable memories" → "the mind withdrawing access to unbearable memories — causal work whose mechanism debt (P-Q3) the article inherits rather than discharges" (+~10); L151 one clause noting analytic idealism also claims DID as its central case (Kastrup), so the data are claimed by three metaphysics (+~15). Skip both if the trim list is spent. Additional trims if needed: L92 "The content channel delivers; the mine-ness it normally carries is decoupled." (−12, restated in the next sentence); L100 "The patient can drive a car, speak their language, perform their job—but cannot recall who they are or what happened to them." (−24, illustrative).

(+~6; this LOWERS the tier). ### ✓ 2026-10-07: phenomenal-concepts-strategy and explanatory-gap state "if PCS fails, then dualism" and "all major versions fail" more strongly than PCS L79 allows — word-neutral calibration
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: /home/andy/unfin/unfinishablemap/obsidian/concepts/phenomenal-concepts-strategy.md
- **Notes**: Research note /home/andy/unfin/unfinishablemap/obsidian/research/rival-explanations-of-the-explanatory-gap-2026-10-03.md (§Corpus Seams): the ignorance hypothesis (concepts/ignorance-hypothesis) and illusionism are further rival explanations of the gap, and Kammerer 2019 concludes that knocking out the fallacy-type PCS accounts gives "indirect weight to the disjunction" of illusion and ignorance accounts — so a PCS failure is not by itself a win for dualism. Loci: (1) phenomenal-concepts-strategy L39 "If PCS fails, the explanatory gap points to something genuinely beyond physics." and L85 "If PCS fails—as Chalmers's master argument suggests—the gap isn't just in how we think: something in consciousness itself resists physical explanation." — scope each so PCS failure leaves the ignorance and illusion explanations standing (zero-word pipes to concepts/ignorance-hypothesis and concepts/illusionism where possible). ### ✓ 2026-10-07: kripke-a-posteriori-necessity-argument L55 reverses Loar — the phenomenal-concepts reply grants that pain lacks a contingent mode of presentation and locates the illusion in conceptual independence
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: /home/andy/unfin/unfinishablemap/obsidian/concepts/kripke-a-posteriori-necessity-argument.md
- **Notes**: L55 says the phenomenal-concepts response "denies the one claim that pain lacks a contingent mode of presentation" and that "phenomenal concepts *are* the mode of presentation Kripke says pain lacks"; L57 repeats that the argument's "force rests entirely on the claim that pain has no mode of presentation, and that claim is precisely what remains contested". Chalmers 2003 ("Consciousness and its Place in Nature", §… on type-B) reports Loar's version the other way round: phenomenal concepts "lack contingent modes of presentation" — Loar grants Kripke's premise about pain and explains the apparent contingency by the conceptual independence of the phenomenal and theoretical concepts (which L55's own next sentences already describe). concepts/phenomenal-concepts-strategy agrees with Chalmers. BEFORE EDITING, read Loar's own text (Loar 1990/1997 "Phenomenal States", Philosophical Perspectives 4: 81–108, rev. in Block, Flanagan & Güzeldere 1997) and quote only what you verify; if Loar's wording supports a "direct" mode of presentation reading, state that precisely instead. Restate L55 and L57 so the reply denies Kripke's INFERENCE (from no contingent mode of presentation to no illusion of contingency), not his premise, and keep "Balog" consistent with phenomenal-concepts-strategy L61 (token experiences as modes of presentation — Balog may differ from Loar; say so if so). LENGTH: 2,227 by analyze_length at 14:45Z (concepts hard 3,500, gate `>=`; headroom 1,272); aim for net ≤ +40 words. Do not edit type-identity-theory (deep-reviewed 14:29Z) or phenomenal-concepts-strategy (headroom 51).

### ✓ 2026-10-07: assent-void says believing-at-will "feels like finding nothing to push on" and that the resistance article's belief observation "has the same source" — but that article says it "encounters something that refuses to move", and noetic-feelings-void makes the feeling of rightness a pre-judgment gate the assent article calls downstream
- **Type**: cross-review
- **File**: obsidian/topics/phenomenology-of-resistance-across-domains.md
- **Notes**: From outer review 2026-10-07 (ChatGPT 5.6 Sol Pro). **Verified on disk this run.** **(a) Resistance vs. nothing-to-push-on.** `phenomenology-of-resistance-across-domains` L62: "Beliefs resist voluntary revision… The felt character is distinctive: the attempt to believe-at-will encounters something that refuses to move." `assent-void` L89: trying to believe at will "feels, not like effortful failure, but like finding nothing to push on", and in the same sentence claims the resistance article's observation "has the same source". The two pages describe the same attempt with opposite phenomenology — felt pushback vs. felt absence — and the assent article papers over it with "same source". The reconciliation the reviewer offers is right and cheap: distinguish (i) the felt frustration of a failed higher-level attempt (what the resistance article records), (ii) the stability mechanism that is not itself felt, and (iii) initial acquisition from later revision — the resistance article is about *revision* of a standing belief, the assent article about *formation*. Install (i)/(iii) as one sentence in the resistance article's L62 paragraph with a piped link to [[assent-void]], and rewrite assent-void L89's "has the same source" to state the acquisition/revision split instead of asserting identity. Do not demote either page's phenomenology on the other's authority — state the asymmetry. **(b) Noetic feelings temporal taxonomy.** `noetic-feelings-void` L72 "Consciousness does not terminate inquiry by argument. It terminates inquiry by feeling", L81 "Feeling of rightness (FOR)… Gates engagement of analytic thought", L87 "Feeling of confidence… Gates commitment to action", L90 Proust: feelings "drive epistemic control beneath reportable judgment". These make rightness/confidence *upstream gates* on judgment; `assent-void` L51 ("Certainty is the *feeling* that accompanies a belief once held… The assent void is upstream of it") and L116 ("the confidence signal downstream of assent") make them *downstream reports*. Both cannot be unqualified. Install, length-neutrally, the four-way taxonomy the reviewer proposes — pre-judgment evidence (FOR as fluency signal, Thompson, already at L98) / constitutive feeling of judging (Smithies 2026, *The Nature of Belief* ch. 11, doi:10.1093/9780197744208.003.0011, verified: belief as "a disposition to feel convinced") / accompanying conviction / post-judgment confidence (Koriat–Fleming metacognition) — as a substitution in noetic-feelings-void's family-list framing sentence, and change assent-void L51/L116 from "downstream" to "downstream *or constitutive*, the classification the noetic-feelings void draws". The assent-void edit is a two-word substitution and must not pre-empt the P2 Seam task's budget. Apply the direct-refutation discipline where the taxonomy forces a choice: if a feeling of rightness gates judgment, it is not a report on a completed assent, and the article should say which cases are which rather than declaring all of them downstream by definition (the reviewer's "immune to counterevidence" charge, §6). Sync Hugo after.

Either add assent-void (and decision-void, if it genuinely carries the shape — check its faces) to both lists with the seam-test obligation, or amend assent-void L55 to say it has *not yet* passed the seam test; the methodology task above is adding a common-cause clause to the Seam Test using assent-void as its worked *failure*, so the honest listing is "candidate, seam test pending". - **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/project/writing-style.md

### ✓ 2026-10-07: assent-void — the Seam fails a common-cause test and names no rival: control and transparency share the truth-norm, and predictive processing / deflationary readings predict all three faces
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/voids/assent-void.md
- **Notes**: From convergent outer reviews (2026-10-07, 2/3 reviewers): [claude, chatgpt]. **Upgraded P2 → P1 by `/combine-outer-reviews`**: both reviewers independently found the Seam asserted rather than shown — Claude: "The control and transparency faces are not independent ... The same truth-norm generates both faces ... Calling the conjunction 'informative rather than one finding stated three ways' is coherence inflation"; ChatGPT: "control, timing and transparency 'conjoin on one event.' At present this is asserted rather than demonstrated ... Four bridge premises are missing" — and both found that involuntary does not entail unfelt (Claude via deflationary readings on which there is no event; ChatGPT via "pain, surprise and recognition" and Smithies 2026 on which the event is felt). Those two rivals are mutually exclusive and both deny the article's unfelt event: name both in the rival paragraph and say what survives either. ChatGPT's alternative remedy (split the article into three) is declined — demote the conjunction to coherence-only inside the existing Seam paragraph instead. Originally from outer review 2026-10-07 (Claude Fable 5.1). Apply the direct-refutation discipline. Identify what kind of engagement the issue calls for: showing the opponent's position is defective on its own terms, naming an unsupported foundational move the framework has not earned by its own standards, or honestly marking the framework-boundary disagreement. Apply the corresponding reply mode in **natural journal-quality prose** — see [[writing-style|the writing-style guide]]'s "Engaging Opponents in Journal-Quality Prose" section. **Do not expose mode labels in the article body.** The classification is editor-internal; it belongs in the refine-draft / deep-review changelog entry, not in the article. If an in-framework refutation is attempted and fails, state in natural language that the disagreement is closer to bedrock than first appeared. **Three findings.** **(a) Common cause.** L83 says the faces are severable and "their co-occurrence in the human case is informative rather than one finding stated three ways", but the article shows severability only for the timing face. Its own SEP source says the truth-aim "forms the backbone for many involuntarist arguments" and lists Shah & Velleman under conceptual involuntarism — so the control and transparency faces have one upstream source, belief's truth-norm. State that plainly and demote the conjunction to coherence-only: at most two independent items co-occur, and one of them (timing) is contested. **(b) The named rival.** The article has zero mentions of predictive processing, precision, Bayesian updating or any of Friston/Hohwy/Clark/Seth (verified by grep). ⚠️ This is **not** the site-wide blind spot the reviewer frames it as — the Map engages Laukkonen/active inference in 38 live pages, including [[predictive-processing]], [[predictive-processing-and-dualism]], [[agency-void]] and [[minimal-consciousness-void]]; that framing is the recurring outer-reviewer false absence already declined upstream. For *this* article the gap is real and the fix is a short paragraph that links those pages rather than rebuilding them: precision-weighted updating predicts the control face (posteriors cannot be set directly, only via action that changes evidence — Pascal's holy water recast), the timing face (prior-weighted encoding is exactly Vorms et al.'s plausibility asymmetry, which L73 already concedes as "a prior-weighted tag"), and the transparency face (first-order posteriors are about the world; confidence is a higher-level precision estimate, the noetic signal). Then say what, if anything, is left over; if nothing is, say so — the article's site-perspective section already has that restraint. Laukkonen, Friston & Chandaria (2025), *Neurosci Biobehav Rev* 176:106296, doi:10.1016/j.neubiorev.2025.106296 (metadata verified). **(c) Deflationary readings.** Mandelbaum (already cited) models belief as "the automatic output of a computationally null belief acquisition reflex"; Carruthers's ISA and illusionism likewise deny there is an *event* whose phenomenal absence needs remarking — sub-personal processes are phenomenally absent as a rule. The phrase "causally central and phenomenally absent" (L41) is arresting only on the prior that causally central events should be phenomenally present, which is the Map's expectation, not the physicalist's. Add one sentence conceding this and flag the discreteness assumption in the opening (L41 "the moment at which…": condition it on belief formation being a discrete transition rather than graded credal updating). **Optional, if budget remains:** one sentence anchoring the title's term — Stoic *synkatathesis* (Epictetus treats assent as voluntary, SEP citing *Discourses* 3.12.14) and Spinoza *Ethics* IIP49 as the historical source of Gilbert's label — linking [[research/voids-reality-feeling-void-2026-02-14]] for Husserl's doxic modality. The Occam paragraph (L107) should either be cut or concede that "introspection misreports architecture" cuts against introspective evidence generally; prefer the concession since the effort-as-evidence tension is an open operator item (NEEDS-HUMAN 2026-08-17). Sync Hugo after. **CONVERGENT ADDENDUM (ChatGPT 5.6 Sol Pro, same cycle, `reviews/outer-review-2026-10-07-chatgpt-5-6-sol-pro.md` §4, §6, §7; improvements 7, 12) — the named rivals this task says the article lacks now have verified 2026 citations; fold them into finding (b)'s rival paragraph rather than adding a section.** **(d)** *The Nature of Belief*, J. Lewis-Jong & E. Schwitzgebel (eds.), Oxford University Press, 2026 (Crossref: issued 2026-02-27, ISBN 9780197744208): **Smithies, D., ch. 11, "Belief as a Feeling of Conviction"**, doi:10.1093/9780197744208.003.0011 — abstract verbatim: belief is "a disposition to feel convinced of a proposition's truth" and "feelings of conviction, whether occurrent or dispositional, are enough for belief when they are subject to doxastic norms of rationality". This is the live counterposition to L51 / L116 "the confidence signal downstream of assent": a belief can be *involuntary and yet felt*, so the control face does not deliver the phenomenal-absence conclusion on its own. One sentence conceding this, in the rival paragraph. **Singh, K., ch. 9, "Belief as Commitment to the Truth"**, doi:10.1093/9780197744208.003.0009 (abstract: "belief is a volitional act, forming a voluntary commitment to truth"; the chapter addresses doxastic voluntarism and Moore's paradox directly) and **Crowe, J. (2026)**, Existential reasons for choosing to believe, *Synthese* 207(6), doi:10.1007/s11229-026-05615-9 (abstract: "a modest defence of DDV") are current defenders against L43 "arguments most of the literature accepts" and L63 "no believer, human or artificial" — name them in one clause where the P1 task installs Booth 2017, so the control face reads "dominant but contested" with a 2026 citation behind "contested". Mugg, J., ch. 10 of the same volume (003.0010), exists, but its abstract concerns architecture and belief-kinds and says nothing about direct control — do not cite it for that. **Budget:** these add ~70 words to the ≤300 budget above; re-measure after the P1 lands and, if the 548-word headroom is gone, file a NEEDS-HUMAN length line rather than cut reviewed prose. **RESCOPE 2026-10-07 07:02Z (driver):** the calibration P1 landed at 07:00Z and the page now measures **2,956/3,000** (gate >=; 43 words of room) — the ≤300-word budget no longer exists. Run this task SUBSTITUTION-ONLY: install the common-cause failure, the named rival(s) and the deflationary readings by replacing the Seam section's existing assertion sentences word-for-word-neutral or negative (the K4 finding is that the Seam is asserted not shown — the assertions to replace are the budget), or, if the content cannot be carried at net <= 0, file the Seam rewrite as NEEDS-HUMAN (condense-first decision) rather than cross 3,000. Items (2)/(3)/(6) of the calibration task already touched the Seam (four-position sentence; Shah & Velleman + Hieronymi sentence pair) — read the live text first and do not undo them. ### ✓ 2026-10-07: assent-void — four calibration errors verified at source: Williams's status, the "both sides grant" clause, "the formation cannot be an act", and the Descartes "introspectively natural" line
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/voids/assent-void.md
- **Notes**: From convergent outer reviews (2026-10-07, 2/3 reviewers): [claude, chatgpt]. Originally from outer review 2026-10-07 (Claude Fable 5.1, hostile-referee audit); the ChatGPT 5.6 Sol Pro leg independently reached items (1), (2), (3) and the stale-bibliography, Evans/Ginet and continued-influence findings — see the convergent addendum at the end. All four defects were verified at the publisher of record this run — do not re-verify, install. **(1) L43 "rest on arguments most of the literature accepts" / L61 "the classic argument".** The article's own SEP source says of Williams: "his argument is widely held to be unsuccessful—at least without modifications" (Winters 1979); Booth (2017, *Ratio* 30(2):107–120) argues belief is only *contingently* involuntary. Restate the control face as resting on the more popular, conceptual route, report the objection, and add Winters 1979 and Booth 2017 to the references. Re-label the face heading so Alston's colony test (SEP: "best-known defender" of *psychological* involuntarism) is not filed under "Conceptual" — "Unexplorable if conceptual involuntarism holds; occluded if only psychological" — and add Alston, W.P. (1988), The deontological conception of epistemic justification, *Philosophical Perspectives* 2:257–299, doi:10.2307/2214077. **(2) L43 "claims only what both sides grant: the first truth-assignment happens below deliberate judgment."** False of the anti-Spinozan side: Hasson, Simmons & Todorov's own conclusion (verbatim, Crossref abstract) is that "it may be possible to suspend belief in comprehended propositions", and Gilbert's Cartesian model places acceptance/rejection *after* an effortful assessment. Replace with a sentence that states the three positions (default acceptance / tag after assessment / no tag assigned) and drops the "both sides" framing; the L73 "what the void claims survives the dispute" paragraph needs the same correction, and cut "the dispute itself is evidence that it is a first-person blank" (dual-task adjudication is routine for sub-personal processes). **(3) L83 "The formation cannot be an act."** Shah & Velleman (2005), the article's transparency source, say verbatim "A judgment is a cognitive mental act of affirming a proposition" and "As an act, mental affirmation is clearly eligible to be an object of deliberation". Scope the sentence: belief as a *state* is not voluntarily adopted, but S&V hold that judgment, the act that issues in it, is an act and a possible object of deliberation. Same paper restricts transparency to deliberation that deploys the concept of belief ("Not all belief-forming processes require the subject to deploy the concept") — narrow L109 "Belief shows up in experience as world and never as state" to doxastic deliberation accordingly. **(4) L93 "Descartes's picture… is the introspectively natural one."** The SEP history has introspection cited on both sides (Holcot "experiences in himself", Hume "daily experience", Reid "carried along by perception, as irresistibly as my body by the earth"), and the article itself uses Alston's introspective test against voluntarism four paragraphs earlier. Delete or rewrite as "introspection has been enlisted on both sides". **Also in the same pass, cheap:** add the two post-2022 timing-face studies — **Nadarevic, L. & Bell, R. (2024)**, Remembering the truth or falsity of advertising claims, *Psychon Bull Rev* 31(5):2323–2331, doi:10.3758/s13423-024-02482-8 (preregistered, N = 208, "memory for truth and falsity did not differ", supports Cartesian) and **Ford, D. & Nadarevic, L. (2025)**, Revisiting the plausibility effect, *J Cogn* 8(1):46, doi:10.5334/joc.459 (registered report; replicates Vorms et al.'s interaction, attributes it to guessing and statement memory). ⚠️ The reviewer wrote "Bell, Nadarevic et al." — the byline is **Nadarevic & Bell**, two authors; use the corrected form. Cite the L99 continued-influence sentence (Connor Desai, Pilditch & Madsen 2020, *Cognition* 205:104453, which also offers the rational-updating rival) or cut it. Delete the embedded editorial instruction at L47 ("the suspension article should carry the point as a conditional") — the cross-review task below carries it out. Reconcile the Agency Void reference date (ref 15 says 2026-02-25; `source-attribution-void` ref says 2026-04-27; the page's `created` is 2026-02-25, so this article is right and the other page is wrong — leave this one, note it for the cross-review). References list is alphabetical; Alston sorts to position 1, Booth to 2, Connor Desai after Boespflug, Ford after Evans, Nadarevic & Bell before Nadarevic & Erdfelder, Winters last before the Map self-cites — renumber and confirm no inline numeric cross-references (none found this run). Sync Hugo after. **CONVERGENT ADDENDUM (ChatGPT 5.6 Sol Pro, same cycle, `reviews/outer-review-2026-10-07-chatgpt-5-6-sol-pro.md` §2 Nadarevic, Hieronymi, Evans/Ginet; improvements 1, 6, 8, 11) — all verified at source this run, install in the same pass:** **(5)** Add **Nadarevic, L. & Erdfelder, E. (2019)**, More evidence against the Spinozan model: Cognitive load diminishes memory for "true" feedback, *Memory & Cognition* 47(7):1386–1400, doi:10.3758/s13421-019-00940-6 (Europe PMC abstract, verbatim: results "clearly contradict the Spinozan model. However, our results are also only partially in line with the predictions of the Cartesian model. For this reason, we suggest a more flexible model that allows for an optional and context-dependent encoding of 'true' tags and 'false' tags"). "Optional" is the operative word: on the authors' own current model some propositions receive *no* first truth-value, which is the direct refutation of L73 "it is assigned below deliberate judgment" — the item (2) rewrite must list a fourth position (no tag assigned / context-dependent) and the 2013 citation must no longer stand as the authors' last word. Reference order (author, then year): Nadarevic & Bell (2024), Nadarevic & Erdfelder (2013), Nadarevic & Erdfelder (2019). **(6)** Hieronymi's own text (*Controlling Attitudes*, eScholarship PDF, grepped): "Bennett's example seems to me a successful counter to Williams' argument, as given" and, on immediacy, "In answering the question positively, one has already, therein, believed. The immediacy of evaluative control is thus not temporal or causal but rather a consequence of the constitutive relation between the commitment to p as true and the belief." So L61 "reaches the same verdict by another route" must say she *concedes Bennett's Credamites against Williams as given* and rests the impossibility on commitment-constitution; and L83/L87 must concede that on her account the conscious answering *is* the believing — there is no earlier hidden event the answer reports on. This is the same correction direction as item (3) (Shah & Velleman's judgment-as-act) and the two should be installed as one sentence pair, not two. **(7)** Refs 3 (Evans 1982, "not checked against the text") and 6 (Ginet 2001, "not examined") ship unverified load-bearing citations, which `project/writing-style` L550 forbids ("'Pending verification' is not a publishable state for a load-bearing citation… do *not* ship the gap as a disclosure"). Verify both at source in this pass — Evans's transparency passage is conventionally located at *The Varieties of Reference* p. 225 (the "third world war" example; confirm the page at source, do not copy this number unverified), Ginet's juror case is in Steup (ed.) 2001 pp. 63–76 — and strip the caveats; if either cannot be reached, demote the sentence it supports rather than keep the disclosure. The ChatGPT review's remaining article items (2 demote the opening universal; 3 terminology; 9 behavioural-vs-phenomenological separation; 10 disagreement-as-evidence; 14 falsification box; 15 artificial-agent universal; 16 continued influence) are either already in this task (10 = cut "the dispute itself is evidence"; 16 = Connor Desai cite-or-cut; 15 = scope L63 "no believer, human or artificial" to what the conceptual argument actually binds) or are absorbed by the Seam task below; do not mint them separately. **SYNTHESIS ADDENDUM (`/combine-outer-reviews` 2026-10-07, cluster K10 — both reviewers, independently):** **(8)** L93–L97 edge phenomenology. Claude: "'Approaching the edge feels like finding that one already believes' carries no citation and no method. It is armchair phenomenology presented as description" (C10: cite or mark as first-person conjecture). ChatGPT: dismissing contemplative counter-reports because "the accuracy of such reports is precisely what is doubtful" is circular — "the article should say that ordinary introspective access is uncertain or uncommon, not impossible." Install as substitution, in the same pass as item (4): mark the headline/rumour examples as illustrative first-person conjecture (one clause), and change the L97 dismissal of contemplative reports from implied impossibility to "uncertain or uncommon; not examined here, and not shown impossible" (one clause). No new citations; length-neutral.

### ✓ 2026-10-07: Scientific-explanation page: correlation is equated with a breach of causal closure (L52), and gap persistence is called a prediction physicalism lacks (L60)
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: /home/andy/unfin/unfinishablemap/obsidian/concepts/consciousness-and-scientific-explanation.md
- **Notes**: (1) L52: "Nothing about the empirical programme of neuroscience requires that consciousness *be* a neural process. It requires only that consciousness and neural processes be systematically related — that the [[causal-closure|causal closure]] of physics is not absolute, or at minimum that non-physical influence operates within the gaps that physics itself leaves open." The dash equates systematic relation with a breach of closure. Correlation is compatible with closure (epiphenomenalism and parallelism keep it), so neuroscience requires only the relation; the closure clause is the Map's further commitment (Tenet 3), and should be stated as that, not as something neuroscience requires. (2) L60: "Dualism does make one additional prediction that physicalism does not … Under physicalism, the gap should eventually close as neuroscience matures." That is Type-C physicalism. Type-B physicalism (Loar, Papineau; Lycan, Tye, Carruthers as Chalmers classes them) also predicts a permanent explanatory gap and explains it by phenomenal concepts, so gap persistence does not separate dualism from physicalism generally. Scope "physicalism" to its promissory (Type-C) form, or restate the point so it does not claim a prediction Type-B shares; see concepts/type-a-type-b-and-type-c-physicalism (routing table) and concepts/neural-correlates-of-consciousness L142 for the correct framing. A piped link to the Type-A/B/C page on existing text costs zero words.

### ✓ 2026-10-07: Link the plurality void and manyism to each other (each bears on the other's central claim; zero links either way)
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: /home/andy/unfin/unfinishablemap/obsidian/voids/plurality-void.md
- **Notes**: voids/plurality-void says we cannot conceive "what it would be like to be us" as a genuinely plural first person (L51). concepts/manyism presents Roelofs' view that many overlapping conscious subjects coexist in one place by sharing experience, and treats it as the strongest constitutive-panpsychist rival on the unity question. Neither page links the other. Install the reciprocal in both directions: at manyism, one sentence noting that experience-sharing posits exactly the plural subjecthood the plurality void finds inconceivable from the inside (whether that tells against manyism or only marks a limit of conception is the open question; state it at that tier, since inconceivability from the inside does not establish impossibility); at plurality-void, a piped link where it discusses plural subjects, naming manyism as the live theory that requires what the void cannot picture. Prefer `[[slug|existing text]]` where an existing phrase fits.

- **Type**: refine-draft
