- **Notes**: **arguments-against-materialism**: §Why Materialism Cannot Close the Gap (L73–82) runs the Type-C replies, and L115 concludes that "physicalism—in all its forms from reductive identity theory to eliminativism to illusionism—cannot account for consciousness". Type-B agrees materialism cannot close the gap and claims to account for consciousness by identity; the page's only Type-B engagement (L55) answers it by the convergence pattern alone. Add one short paragraph giving the Type-B reply and the Map's answer at its real tier (compatibility; Tenet 5 against the simplicity argument for the identity), and scope L115 to what the page has shown. **consciousness-defeats-explanation L142**: "Physicalism must instead either treat the failure as temporary … or, with the mysterian, as permanent but epistemic." L65 correctly defines the JND as "the minimum stimulus change detectable at least half the time"; L67 then treats it as a hard phenomenal border with "no intermediate state". Carry the consequence into the Dualism paragraph L92, which currently leans on the retired contrast. ### ✓ 2026-10-06: `concepts/egocentric-presentism` — argumentative repairs: strongest deflationist horn, "has shown" → conditional, "by construction" → "represented", immeasurable→nonphysical inference vs Tenet 3, fragmentalist exit as promissory note (outer-review 2026-10-05)
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/egocentric-presentism.md
- **Notes**: From outer review 2026-10-05 (judgement items, not errors). **Coordination: run AFTER the P1 verified-errors task on this file has landed — re-read the file first; line numbers below are from the 2026-09-28 text.** **Headroom 2026-10-06: 2255/3500 before the P1 pass; budget ~600 words here, re-measure first.** Apply the direct-refutation discipline throughout: identify what kind of engagement each issue calls for — showing the opponent's position is defective on its own terms, naming an unsupported foundational move the framework has not earned by its own standards, or honestly marking the framework-boundary disagreement — and apply the corresponding reply mode in **natural journal-quality prose** (see [[writing-style|the writing-style guide]]'s "Engaging Opponents in Journal-Quality Prose"); **do not expose mode labels in the article body**; if an in-framework refutation fails, say in natural language that the disagreement is closer to bedrock than it first appeared. Items: (1) **The deflationist horn in its strongest form** (reviewer §3.1 — "the strongest omission"). Perry and Lewis show a complete impersonal description leaves an agent unable to act until she self-locates; it does not follow that reality contains an extra primitive fact. Lewis's property-self-ascription and Perry's action-guiding modes of presentation are the countermodel. State why the Map thinks the move from epistemic indispensability to ontological irreducibility is earned — or mark it as the bedrock disagreement it may be (the 2026-07-18 and 09-28 stability notes already record "bedrock disagreement with physicalists and List"; this item is about *saying so in the article at the point where L32 and L54 lean on List*). (2) **L32 "Christian List has shown"** → conditional: *if* first-personal facts are irreducible, more than one subject exists, facts are globally compossible and there is one world, the four conflict; List supplies a separate defence of the irreducibility premise, which a Perry–Lewis deflationist rejects. (3) **L63 "by construction" / "answered"** → "represented by a primitive first-personal fact": List is tentative, and a primitive fact *encodes* the vertiginous datum rather than explaining why this subject-world is present (reviewer §3.4; the same point applies to the Map's own void-as-primitive stance, so phrase it symmetrically). (4) **L79 immeasurable → non-physical.** "no instrument detects it … non-physical" needs a criterion of the physical; indexical inaccessibility, privacy and causal screening are all compatible with physicality. And there is a Tenet 3 tension the reviewer is right about: if presence has no possible third-personal footprint it does no work for bidirectional interaction; if it does, "immeasurable" must be weakened. Write the tension down honestly rather than resolving it by stipulation. (5) **L77 fragmentalist exit.** The Map's "may instead relinquish non-fragmentation" is a promissory note: name the costs (incompatible facts in one reality; a compossibility relation; whether any fragment is privileged — an *egalitarian* fragmentalism leaves no uniquely present "I", which is in tension with the Map's primitive-"I" language) and point to the live debate: Merlo, "Fragmentalism We Can Believe In", *PQ* 73(1) 184 (10.1093/pq/pqac005; tensed-belief-explosion objection) vs Loss, "Don't Stop Believing", *PQ* 74(3) 1007–1015 (10.1093/pq/pqad008); Lipman, *Standpoints: Time and Subjectivity* (OUP 2026, 10.1093/9780198921318.001.0001) for egalitarian standpoint pluralism; Fine, "The Reality of Tense", *Synthese* 150(3) 399–414 (**2006**) or "Tense and Reality" in *Modality and Tense* (OUP 2005). **Existence of these works is verified; their contents were NOT read by the driver — read before characterising any of them.** (6) **Temporal analogue** (L30, L40): say whether the intended analogue is presentism (only the present exists) or tense realism / moving spotlight (past and future exist, one time is primitively present) — Hare's and Conitzer's own caution; egocentric presentism, with other subjects real, structurally resembles the latter. (7) **L44–46 perspectival realism** is "weaker"/"fallback" in the Map's estimation; mark it as the Map's ranking, and if the pass cites Hare 2010 (*Philosophy Compass* 5(9) 760–769) verify first the reviewer's claim that Hare calls perspectival realism in important respects the better view and supplies point-of-view operators for other standpoints' truths (**unverified lead**). Sync Hugo. Update `ai_modified`.

### ✓ 2026-10-06: `concepts/indexical-knowledge-and-identity` L54 misstates Perry's sugar case — Perry did not believe "Perry is making a mess" (outer-review 2026-10-05, verified against Perry 1979 / SEP)
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/indexical-knowledge-and-identity.md
- **Notes**: From outer review 2026-10-05; **verified by the driver**. **Headroom measured 2026-10-06: 2740 words against concepts hard 3500 (usable ceiling 3499) — ~750 words of room; the fix is sentence-level.** L54 reads: "But Perry *did* believe that Perry was making a mess—he just didn't connect this to himself." In Perry 1979 what Perry already believed was that **"the shopper with the torn sack" was making a mess**; the de dicto proposition "John Perry is making a mess" appears only in his separate **amnesia** variant, where he *might* believe it without the de se belief "I am making a mess". L54 collapses the two cases, and the L52–56 argument ("If 'I am making the mess' were merely another way of representing 'Perry is making the mess'…") should be rebuilt on the right contrast: extensional truth (the shopper = Perry), de dicto belief (the shopper / John Perry is making a mess), de se self-ascription (I am). Either use the amnesia variant explicitly and label it, or stay with the torn-sack description. **Secondary, same pass, length-neutral where possible**: (a) the reviewer notes that **Lewis 1979 analyses de se knowledge as self-ascription of *properties*** and is therefore one of the strongest *deflationary* countermodels to a primitive first-personal fact — the article (L84 "Perry's case and Lewis's Two Gods establish this convincingly", and the Occam's-razor paragraph at L158) recruits Lewis as support for metaphysical first-personality without stating that his own treatment needs no extra fact. Add one honest sentence marking Lewis's account as a serious competitor the Map must answer, not an ally. (b) L76 "**Phenomenal indexical knowledge**" and its application to Mary (L106) is a Map synthesis, not Perry's or Lewis's result — label it as the Map's extension in one clause. Apply the direct-refutation discipline to (a): identify what kind of engagement the issue calls for — showing the opponent's position is defective on its own terms, naming an unsupported foundational move, or honestly marking the framework-boundary disagreement — in natural journal-quality prose per [[writing-style|the writing-style guide]]'s "Engaging Opponents in Journal-Quality Prose" section; do not expose mode labels in the article body. Sync Hugo. Update `ai_modified`.

### ✓ 2026-10-06: `concepts/egocentric-presentism` — three verified source errors (McDaniel/Markosian swap, Conitzer "decision-theoretic", surviving "preferred" at L32) plus the consciousness/non-solipsism equivocation (outer-review 2026-10-05, ChatGPT 5.6 Sol Pro)
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/egocentric-presentism.md
- **Notes**: From outer review 2026-10-05. **All four items below were verified against primary sources by the driver (see the review's Verification Notes) — do not re-litigate, fix.** **Headroom measured 2026-10-06: `analyze_length` 2255 words against concepts soft 2500 / hard 3500 (gate `>=`, usable ceiling 3499) — roughly 1,200 words of room; this pass should need under 400.** (1) **L69 attribution swap.** The sentence credits Kris McDaniel (2012) with arguing that "the evidence supports presence being a two-place relation rather than a monadic property". That is **Markosian's** claim — his review (hosted PDF, text-extracted): "presence is a two-place relation between an experience or an object and a conscious organism"; he also asks what "metaphysical mechanism or backstory" attaches presence to you "rather than someone else" (the L69 Markosian clause already has this half right). McDaniel's *Ethics* 122(2) 403–410 study makes **different** objections, verified in the PDF: (a) presence does not track awareness — unnoticed parts and "electrons" of a present table are present though not perceptual objects, so Hare's "Insight 2 is false"; (b) presence may be one *determinate* of a determinable "presence*", one per point of view, which would leave every subject metaphysically special in its own way and undercut Hare's axiological "Harmony"; (c) Hare's treatment of "I" as a "nonrigid definite description that refers to whoever it is that has present" perceptual objects. Reassign the two-place claim to Markosian and give McDaniel one accurate sentence or two (objections a–c). (2) **L71 Conitzer.** Replace "independently reconstructs Hare's self–time parity from a decision-theoretic starting point" — the Dialectica paper's abstract (arXiv 2008.13207) says he argues that **A-theorists** should accept a metaphysically distinguished "I" because "key arguments in favor of the A-theory are more effective as arguments in favor of the resulting combined position, and key arguments against the A-theory are ineffective against the combined position"; he names Hare's two papers and book explicitly, so "independently" goes too. (3) **L32 "List's own preferred resolution"** — the 2026-09-28 deep review changed only the L63 instance to "tentatively prefers"; propagate the tentative wording to L32 (List: "tentative preference for the fourth route", not defended in the quadrilemma paper). (4) **Consciousness/non-solipsism.** L30 says "Other people are genuinely conscious"; L55 defines non-solipsism as "more than one conscious subject is real"; L61 says egocentric presentism drops it. State the distinction the L61 gloss already gestures at **once, at the definition**: other subjects are conscious *relative to their own standpoints* while only one subject's experiences instantiate presence *simpliciter* — that is the technical sense in which List files Hare under the solipsist horn. **Lead only — verify before attributing**: the reviewer says Builes, "Eight Arguments for First-Person Realism" (*Philosophy Compass* 19(1), 10.1111/phc3.12959 — exists) states the standpoint-relative/simpliciter formulation; read it before citing it for that. (5) **References**: Markosian's review appeared in *The Philosophical Review* 123 (2014) 360–366 (the PDF's own header line) — replace the 2015/PhilArchive-only entry; the "Are You Special?" title was **not** seen in the PDF (its title line is "Review of Caspar Hare's *On Myself…*"), so check PhilArchive MAROMA-6 before keeping it. Add Hare 2010, "Realism About Tense and Perspective", *Philosophy Compass* 5(9) 760–769, 10.1111/j.1747-9991.2010.00325.x, if the pass cites it (it is the primary source for perspectival realism). (6) **Zero-cost**: L75 "load-bearing" is on the writing-style overused list; replace with a plain word. **Coordination**: a P2 argumentative task on the same file follows this one — do NOT attempt the deflationism/fragmentalism work here; keep this pass to the verified fixes. Sync Hugo after (both trees carry the defects). Update `ai_modified`.
